# Implementation source log

This log continues the spec's source log (Appendix A of `docs/spec/openftgs-spec.md`) for the implementation phase. Every source consulted gets one row: what it was and what was taken from it. Reading this repository and the spec needs no row.

If a forbidden source is met by accident, its row starts with **Incident** and says what was seen, that reading stopped, and that nothing was taken.

| # | Date | Source | What was taken |

| 6 | 2026-10-11 | gsplat 1.5.3 as installed from PyPI (Apache-2.0; `gsplat-1.5.3.dist-info/licenses/LICENSE` read first): `gsplat/cuda/_torch_impl.py`, `_eval_sh_bases_fast` and `_spherical_harmonics`; `gsplat/cuda/_wrapper.py`, the `spherical_harmonics` signature and docstring | The SH basis is ordered by band l and, within a band, m = −l … l; signs keep the Condon–Shortley phase (l = 1 is C₁·(−y, z, −x); the odd-m terms of l = 2 and l = 3 are negative); directions are normalized inside; `spherical_harmonics(degrees_to_use, dirs [..., 3], coeffs [..., K, 3])` returns the plain sum over bases, without the +0.5 offset or a clamp. No code copied: `openftgs.sh` is written from the Cartesian table of the real SH, not from gsplat's recurrence (from #15: M2 · Motion, temporal opacity and spherical harmonics) |

Neither project's documentation nor source was opened (from #15: M2 · Motion, temporal opacity and spherical harmonics) |

the exported stage functions and their signatures; the culls in order (depth, `det <= 0`, opacity below `ALPHA_THRESHOLD` = `1.f/255.f`), the extent `min(3.33, sqrt(2·__logf(opacity/ALPHA_THRESHOLD)))`, `ceilf` radii, the cull when both radii are ≤ `radius_clip`, the off-image test; unpacked `means2d` is allocated with `at::empty` and written only for rows that pass every cull; packed rows are written camera-major in ascending row order by a block scan, with int64 ids; `rasterize_to_pixels` checks the background against `means2d.shape[:-2]`, which rejects a [C, 3] background in packed mode (Q-38); the forward kernel's 0.999 clamp with `__expf`, skip and exclusive stop; the Jacobian-clamp and α_max gradients pass inclusively at the bound; the projection's backward gives no opacity gradient; the SH backward returns the direction gradient; the CUDA code is JIT-built with fast math unless `NO_FAST_MATH=1`, which is why the backends differ in the last bits (Q-39) (from #44: M5 · gsplat renderer) |

Parallel rays through the principal point give a null vector with h₄ = 0 exactly; through other pixels, h₄ of about 10⁻¹⁶ and a finite point about 10¹⁵ away; a zero matrix gives (0, 0, 0, 1). | T7.1 | 2026-11-15 | arXiv abstract page https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.17888 (2D Gaussian Splatting, Huang et al.; v3 of 2025-02-22, whose comment says Eq. 7 was corrected) | Title, authors, version history and abstract: the volume of a 3D Gaussian is collapsed into an oriented planar disk, splatted by a ray–splat intersection, with a depth distortion term and a normal consistency term added to the loss; it names no pretrained network (#7) |
| T7.2 | 2026-11-15 | arXiv abstract pages https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.17774 (High-quality Surface Reconstruction using Gaussian Surfels, Dai et al., v2), https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.12775 (SuGaR, Guédon and Lepetit, v3), https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.06521 (PGSR, Chen et al., v2), https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.01467 (RaDe-GS, Zhang et al., v2) and https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.10772 (Gaussian Opacity Fields, Yu et al., v2), the five other search seeds of family A, their IDs taken from an arXiv title search and one web search | Titles, authors, versions and abstracts. Gaussian Surfels: the z-scale of a 3D Gaussian is set to 0 and the local z-axis taken as the normal, with a normal–depth consistency loss, monocular normal priors and foreground masks. SuGaR: a regularization term that aligns the Gaussians with the surface, then Poisson meshing and an optional refinement that binds Gaussians to the mesh. PGSR: a depth rendered from the distance of each Gaussian's plane to the camera and a normal map, with single-view geometric, multi-view photometric and multi-view geometric regularization and an exposure model. RaDe-GS: rasterized depth and normal maps for general 3D Gaussians. Gaussian Opacity Fields: an opacity field from ray-traced volume rendering of 3D Gaussians, the normal of the ray–Gaussian intersection plane as the surface normal, and meshing by Marching Tetrahedra (#7) |
| T7.3 | 2026-11-15 | arXiv HTML https://arxiv.org/html/2403.17888 (2D Gaussian Splatting, v3): sections 4.1, 5, 6.1, 6.3 and 7 ("Limitations"), Tables 1, 3, 4, 5 and 6, and Appendices A to C, read as text in the scratch directory | The primitive is a disk whose rotation holds two tangent vectors and their cross product, the normal, with two scales; a pixel's depth is where its ray meets the disk. The depth distortion term is written with absolute differences in the main text (Eq. 13) and implemented with squared differences of the depths mapped to NDC, summed over ordered pairs with running sums in the kernel (Appendix A, Eq. 17). The normal consistency term weights 1 − nᵢᵀN by the blending weights, N from finite differences of the depth points and nᵢ the splat's normal turned to the camera (Eqs. 14, 15); weights 1000 or 100 and 0.05. Mean and median depth as Appendix B defines them. The ablation on DTU (Table 5) and the additional baselines (Table 6), among them 3DGS's rasterization with one scale set to 10⁻⁶; the training times; the limitations (#7) |
| T7.4 | 2026-11-15 | arXiv HTML https://arxiv.org/html/2404.17774 (High-quality Surface Reconstruction using Gaussian Surfels, v2): sections 3.2 to 3.5, 4 ("Ablations") and 5 ("Limitations"), Tables 1, 3 and 4 | The third scale is fixed at 0 and the normal is the rotation's third column; the photometric loss then has no gradient on that column, which the depth–normal consistency loss supplies. Depth per pixel and primitive is the centre's depth plus a slope term from a Taylor expansion of the ray–ellipse intersection (Eq. 7); depth and normal are blended and divided by the accumulated weight (Eq. 6). The five loss terms and their weights: photometric, depth–normal consistency (Eq. 10), a normal prior from Omnidata with a smoothness term (Eq. 11), an opacity term that pushes opacities to 0 or 1 (Eq. 12) and a mask term. Random positions and rotations at the start; pruning of points that receive no gradient; volumetric cutting after training. Table 4's ablation, Table 3's sparse setting, the training times, the limitations (#7) |

The schedule: 7,000 iterations without it, 2,000 with an entropy loss on opacities, pruning below 0.5, then 6,000 with it; 15 to 45 minutes a scene on one V100. | T7.15 | 2026-11-15 | arXiv abstract pages opened for family A's search rounds 1 and 2 (their IDs from a web search's result titles and an arXiv title search), logged on resuming after an interruption: https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08093 (MVGSR), https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.06269 (TriaGS), https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.20331 (GVGS), https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20687 (TopoSurfel), https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.17067 (Geometry Field Splatting with Gaussian Surfels), https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.19454 (GausSurf) and https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.15400 (SolidGS) | Titles, dates and abstracts. MVGSR: surface reconstruction among dynamic objects and distractors, with distractor masks from multi-view feature consistency, a pruning measure from multi-view contributions and a multi-view consistency loss. TriaGS (2025-12): a rendered 3D point is penalized for its distance to a consensus point re-triangulated from a bundle of neighbouring views; DTU Chamfer distance 0.50 mm. GVGS (2026-01): visibility modelled per Gaussian primitive, aggregated across views, for a multi-view geometric consistency, and a block-wise affine alignment of monocular depth. TopoSurfel (2026-08): a proxy mesh extracted by differentiable iso-surfacing guides the surfels' normals and their density control. Geometry Field Splatting: a rendering algorithm that splats a geometry field with Gaussian surfels. GausSurf: patch-match multi-view stereo guides texture-rich areas in an iterative scheme, normal priors the texture-less ones. SolidGS: a more solid kernel function for sparse views, with geometric regularization and monocular normals (#7) |
| T7.16 | 2026-11-15 | arXiv abstract pages opened for search rounds 3 and 4, logged on resuming: https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.11324 (GeoGaussian, ECCV 2024), https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.07499 (Trim 3D Gaussian Splatting), https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.07337 (From Blobs to Spokes, whose method is named Gaussian Wrapping), https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.05075 (GeoSplat) and https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.17547 (G3Splat) | Titles, dates and abstracts. GeoGaussian: thin Gaussians initialized aligned with surfaces seen in the point cloud, a densification that passes the property on, and explicit geometry constraints. TrimGS: Gaussians are removed by their contribution, scales are kept small, and the method combines with 3DGS and 2DGS. Gaussian Wrapping (2026-04): a learnable oriented normal per Gaussian, an occupancy field, a consistency loss, a densification that closes holes, and a rasterizer changed to output depth as an isosurface. GeoSplat: normals and curvatures estimated from local structure drive initialization, gradient updates and densification, with scales initialized from principal curvatures. G3Splat: priors for feed-forward splatting networks, not for per-scene training (#7) |
| T7.17 | 2026-11-15 | arXiv abstract pages opened for search rounds 5 and 6, logged on resuming: https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.14633 (SparseSurf, AAAI 2026), https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20378 (Sparse2DGS, CVPR 2025), https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.02893 (Intrinsic Geometry-Appearance Consistency Optimization for Sparse-View Gaussian Splatting) and https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.13508 (Multiview Geometric Regularization of Gaussian Splatting for Accurate Radiance Fields, Kim et al., EGSR 2025) | Titles, dates and abstracts. SparseSurf (2025-11): says the stronger anisotropy of flattened Gaussians worsens overfitting when views are sparse; its remedy aligns geometry with a stereo network's depth and adds a feature consistency over training and unseen views. Sparse2DGS: initialization from learned multi-view stereo. Kim et al.: multi-view stereo depth, colour and normal constraints in initialization and optimization, with a relative depth loss on median depth weighted by an uncertainty, aimed at scenes whose colour varies between viewpoints. The page of 2603.02893 shows, under that title, the abstract of another work, on reconstructing a person from one image through a multi-view diffusion model (it names itself MVD-HuGaS); nothing was taken from it but that mismatch (#7) |
